2.   RECOGNITION  
A.  Red Cross District of Distinction Award 
This evening, we would like to welcome Mr. Leonard Rezmierski , Michigan 
American Red Cross Board Member, and Mrs. Kelly DeLong, Michigan 
American Red Cross Donor Recruitment Representative, who are here with us 
to present the Troy School District Board of Education with a “Certificate of 
Recognition” for supporting the Michigan Red Cross programs.    
Recommended Action:  Invite Mr. Rezmierski and Mrs. DeLong to the  
Podium to present the certificate.

 5. C.  08.20.19 
5. CURRICULUM 
C. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c 
     9th Grade for 2020 – 2021 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seats will be available for the 2020-2021 school year for grade 
9 at the International Academy East.  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105c resolution for 85 non-resident 
9th grade students  who reside outside of Oakland County , if that intermediate 
school district is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District 
boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105c  of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
Schools of Choice program for the 2020-2021 school year for 85 non-resident 9th 
grade students who reside outside of Oakland  County, if that Intermediate 
School District is contiguous wi th the Oakland Intermediate School District 
boundaries for the purposes of attending the International Academy East 
program located in the Troy School District. 
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

Dear Board Presidents, 
Sending at the request of the Board of Directors, Oakland County School Boards Association 
(OCSBA)… 
The OCSBA Legislative Committee, comprised of Trustees from each of our Member School 
Districts, has reviewed and revised their Legislative Priorities.  These have been formatted as a 
Resolution that is now ready for each Member School District to review and vote on at their next 
meeting.    
The voting window for your Board of Education to review and vote on this Resolution is August 
1st through September 15th.  Resolutions that are approved by at least nineteen Member 
School Districts shall be the official position of the OCSBA.  The results of the vote will be 
presented at the September 24th OCSBA meeting.  Please email your Resolution, with the 
results filled in, by September 15th to Birgit McQuiston:  birgit.mcquiston@lok12.org  
 If your school district is in need of further direction, please do not hesitate to contact one of our 
Resolutions and Bylaws Committee Members: 
Carol Finkelstein  carol.finkelstein@wbsd.org  248-321-7999 
Marc Katz  marcgoblue1@gmail.com  
Birgit McQuiston  birgit.mcquiston@lok12.org 
Per the OCSBA Bylaws, Article II, a part of our purpose is to present a strong force representing 
the view of school boards in Oakland County to the political representatives serving the area.  
As Board of Education Trustees we are called to be advocates for education.  By uniting our 
voices through the OCSBA Resolutions, our advocacy impact is multiplied exponentially.  
Thank you in advance for your participation! 
Respectfully, 
Birgit McQuiston 
Secretary, Board of Directors, Oakland County School Board Association

6. A. PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  SUPERINTENDENT’S CONTRACT EXTENSION  
The Board recently completed Superintendent Dr. Richard Machesky’s 
performance evaluation and would like to share the follo wing assessment 
with the community. 
Dr. Machesky has completed his fourth year as a highly effective 
superintendent and exceeded expectations in all evaluation categories.  
Dr. Machesky’s leadership continues to embody and support an exemplary 
learning co mmunity that supports innovation and is committed to 
continuous improvement to ensure all students learn.  
His continued support and promotion of oneTROY, of the school board 
and this district’s mission, vision, goals, and beliefs have made Troy a 
destination district as we continue our journey to world class. 
It is appropriate at this time for the Board to extend Dr. Machesky’s 
contract through June 30, 2024. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by        
 Supported by       
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Troy Board of Education extends 
the contract of the Superintendent of Schools  as mentioned above.  This 
contract will be in effect from July 1, 20 19 through June 30, 20 24, without 
tenure in the administrative position a nd subject to provisions of the written 
Agreement.

10.  OTHER 
  CONSIDERATION:      Change January 2020 Board Meeting Date  
                      to Third Tuesday of the Month 
  The January 14, 20 20, Board of Education Organizational and 
Regular Meeting date was  inadvertently s cheduled for the second 
Tuesday of the month.  The Troy School Board Meetings are held on 
the third Tuesday of each month, so it is necessary for the Board of 
Education to approve changing the date of this meeting to the third 
Tuesday of the month, which is January 21, 2020.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be It Therefore Resolved  that the Board of Education takes action to change 
the date of the January 14, 2020, Board of Educat ion meeting to the third 
Tuesday of the month, which is January 21, 2020.

11. A. CLOSED SESSION 
CONSIDERATION:  Attorney – Client Privilege 
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session  to discuss an attorney-
client privilege. Following the passage of the resolution, the Board of Education will go 
into closed session.  No additional business will take place after this time, other than 
to officially adjourn the meeting. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved  that, as permitted in the Michigan Open Meetings Act, 
Public Act 267, Section 8 (c), the Troy Board of Education shall go into closed session 
to discuss an attorney – client privilege.
